# Sharp mass bounds for the two-dimensional O(4) model

Coordinate `EN-115-000-0020-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-PH-M-0407` | `PM-215.4` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Sharp-mass-bounds-for-the-two-dimensional-O4-model-September-23-2026` | date 2026-09-23 | derived from the openai/math preprint Sharp-mass-bounds-for-the-two-dimensional-O4-model-September-23-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove sharp mass bounds for the two-dimensional nearest-neighbor $`O(4)`$ model. For all sufficiently large inverse couplings <i>β</i>, the full transfer gap, including rotation-invariant local-observable sectors, is bounded above and below by positive multiples of $`\\sqrt\\beta e^{-\\pi\\beta}`$. We also prove that the model has a unique periodic local limit and a positive full gap at every finite <i>β</i> &gt; 0, resolving the all-temperature lattice mass-generation conjecture for this periodic state. Under the stated cutoff scaling, the mass bounds are uniform in independently fixed physical units. A continuum spectral interpretation requires separate convergence hypotheses for the transfer semigroup and the block observables.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove sharp mass bounds for the two-dimensional nearest-neighbor $`O(4)`$ model. For all sufficiently large inverse couplings <i>β</i>, the full transfer gap, including rotation-invariant local-observable sectors, is bounded above and below by positive multiples of $`\sqrt\beta e^{-\pi\beta}`$. We also prove that the model has a unique periodic local limit and a positive full gap at every finite <i>β</i> &gt; 0, resolving the all-temperature lattice mass-generation conjecture for this periodic state. Under the stated cutoff scaling, the mass bounds are uniform in independently fixed physical units. A continuum spectral interpretation requires separate convergence hypotheses for the transfer semigroup and the block observables.

- **claim-main-sharp-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are finite constants $\beta_0,c,C$, with $0<c<C$, such that for every $\beta\ge\beta_0$ the periodic square measures have a unique local limit, and its full transfer gap satisfies $$\label{eq:target} c\sqrt\beta\,e^{-\pi\beta}\le m_{\mathrm{lat}}(\beta) \le C\sqrt\beta\,e^{-\pi\beta}.$$ There are also a fixed dyadic blocking factor $L$, a fixed large terminal coupling $H$, and finite integers $N_0,M_0$ with the following property. For every $N\ge N_0$ one can choose a bare coupling $\beta_N$ whose exact $N$-step block transformation has terminal kinetic coupling $H$, and $$\label{eq:depth-bounds} \frac{\log2}{M_0L^N}\le m_{\mathrm{lat}}(\beta_N) \le\frac{\log8}{L^N}.$$ The same bounds hold uniformly for terminal couplings in a fixed small interval about $H$, after increasing $M_0$ and $N_0$ if necessary.
  - Explanation: For large bare inverse coupling the periodic measures have a unique local limit, and the full transfer gap is bounded above and below by fixed multiples of the predicted asymptotic-freedom scale. A matching depth-indexed bound holds at the blocking scale.
- **claim-gap-every-temperature** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-all-temperature)): For every finite $\beta>0$, the measures <a href="#eq:measure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:measure">[eq:measure]</a> on periodic squares have a unique local limit. There are absolute constants $c,C>0$ such that its full transfer gap obeys $$m_{\mathrm{lat}}(\beta)\ge c\exp\{-\pi\beta- C\sqrt{(1+\beta)\log(2+\beta)}\}>0.$$
  - Explanation: At every finite positive inverse coupling the periodic limit is unique and the full gap is strictly positive, with an explicit lower bound that loses a subexponential factor relative to the sharp scale.
- **claim-trace-tail-squaring** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-squaring)): The excited trace tail $$s_n(w)=\sum_{i\ge2}\left(\frac{\lambda_i(w)}{\lambda_1(w)}\right)^n$$ satisfies $s_n(w)\le e^{p(n,w)}-1$. If $p(n,w)\le1/4$, then $$\label{eq:squaring} \left(\frac{\lambda_2(w)}{\lambda_1(w)}\right)^n\le2p(n,w), \qquad p(2n,w)\le4p(n,w)^2.$$ The analogous statements hold for $q$ when the other period is doubled.
  - Explanation: The excited trace tail is controlled by the defect of the doubled partition function, and when that defect is small the defect itself contracts quadratically under time doubling.
- **claim-finite-volume-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-criterion)): Let $n\ge4$ be even. If $$\Delta_\beta(n)\le\eta_*:=2^{-10}, \label{eq:small-test}$$ then the transfer operators on circumferences $2^kn$, $k\ge0$, satisfy $$\frac{\lambda_2(2^kn)}{\lambda_1(2^kn)} \le e^{-(\log2)/n}. \label{eq:width-gap}$$ Every local limit of the square tori with sides $2^kn$ has full gap at least $(\log2)/n$. In particular, if the periodic infinite-plane state is unique, then $$\label{eq:criterion-gap} m_{\mathrm{lat}}(\beta)\ge\frac{\log2}{n}.$$
  - Explanation: Once the square-box defect on one even torus is below a fixed threshold, the transfer eigenvalue ratio is uniformly controlled on all dyadically enlarged circumferences and the full gap is bounded below by a multiple of the inverse side length.
- **claim-preliminary-mixing** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-preliminary)): There are fixed positive constants $C,c,p$ such that, on setting $$\Xi(\beta)=\exp\left[\pi\beta+ C\sqrt{(1+\beta)\log(2+\beta)}\right], \label{eq:pre:Xi}$$ the following hold for every $\beta\geq0$.* *For every unpinned rectangular torus with each side at least $C\Xi(\beta)$, and every choice $0\leq\beta_e\leq\beta$, local observables $F,G$ supported on $A,A'$ at distance $d$ satisfy $$\|\operatorname{Cov}(F,G)\|\leq C(1+\beta+\|A\|+\|A'\|+d)^{20} L_FL_G e^{-cd/\Xi(\beta)}. \label{eq:pre:theorem-mixing}$$ The same bound holds on every unpinned free subgraph, with ambient lattice distance. In the homogeneous model the local limit of periodic square measures is unique.* *Let $Z_\beta(n,m)$ be the partition function of the homogeneous $n$-by-$m$ torus. For every even $n\geq C\Xi(\beta)$, $$0\leq\Delta_\beta(n):=4\log Z_\beta(n,n)-\log Z_\beta(2n,2n) \leq C(1+\beta)^pn^p e^{-cn/\Xi(\beta)}. \label{eq:pre:theorem-delta}$$
  - Explanation: Local Lipschitz observables on unpinned tori and free subgraphs decorrelate exponentially at a length scale slightly above the predicted correlation scale, giving uniqueness of the periodic limit and a bound on the square-box defect.
- **claim-current-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-current-moments)): For every fixed $1\leq p<\infty$,* *$$\\|X_Q(F)\\|_p\leq C_p(1+b)\log(2+S)\\|F\\|_{C^1}. \label{eq:pre:2-2}$$* *Here and subsequently the derivative norm is in the indicated rescaled coordinates. The constants are independent of pins, signs, twists, and side length.
  - Explanation: The current observable on a pinned square has moments of every fixed order that grow only logarithmically in the side length, uniformly in pins, signs and twists.
- **claim-free-precision-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-free-bounds)): For all sufficiently large $L$, the following bounds hold with constants independent of $h$ and $L$. The functions $K_h$ are analytic and uniformly bounded on a common complex neighborhood of the momentum torus, $$cD(p)\le K_h(p)\le CD(p)\quad(p\text{ real}),\qquad K_h(p)-D(p)=O(\|p\|^4).$$ Their differences between depths $\bar h\ge h$ have the corresponding analytic bounds multiplied by $L^{-h}$. There are real-kernel, self-adjoint edge operators $B_h$, with analytic Hermitian Fourier symbols on real momentum, such that $$K_h=d^*B_hd,\qquad cI\le B_h\le CI,\qquad B_h(0)=I,$$ whose inverses and history differences satisfy the same bounds.* *Writing $K=K_h$ and $K'=K_{h+1}$, the conditional mean operator is $$\label{free:P} P_l=L^{-2}K(k_l)^{-1}b(k_l)^*K'(p).$$ It obeys $KP=Q^*K'$ and $a(I-QP)=K'$, preserves constants and centered affine fields, and, for every fixed integer $r\ge0$, $$\label{free:derivative} \sup_x\sum_y e^{c\|x/L-y\|}\|\nabla_f^rP(x,y)\|\le C_rL^{-r}.$$ Here $\nabla_f$ is a nearest-neighbor difference on the fine lattice. The conditional covariance $$C_h=(K_h+aQ^*Q)^{-1} \label{free:C}$$ has exponentially decaying kernels in coarse units, with constants allowed to depend on the fixed $L$. All these assertions retain the factor $L^{-h}$ in history differences.
  - Explanation: The iterated free precisions stay uniformly elliptic and analytic on a fixed complex neighborhood, match the lattice Laplacian to fourth order, admit an edge lift, and the conditional mean kernel decays exponentially with the stated difference bounds.
- **claim-free-positive-completion** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-free-stack)): One can choose a common $c_0>0$ and edge operators $\ell_h$ such that $$\mathsf A_h=\binom{\sqrt{c_0}I}{\ell_h},\qquad X_h=\mathsf A_hd,$$ give the free precision $X_h^*X_h=K_h$. There are real operators $\mathcal M_h,\mathcal N_h$ satisfying $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal M_h^*\mathcal M_h+\mathcal N_h^*\mathcal N_h&=I, \label{free:isometry}\\ \mathcal M_h^*(X_hu,\sqrt a(V-Qu))&=X_{h+1}V, \label{free:ambient}\\ \mathcal M_hX_{h+1}&=(X_hP,\sqrt a(I-QP)), \label{free:harmonic}\\ \mathcal N_hX_{h+1}&=0. \label{free:null} \end{aligned}$$ All kernels have exponential moments and the history bounds of Lemma <a href="#free:bounds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="free:bounds">7</a>. The fine-output part of $\mathcal M_h$ has weighted row norm $C/L$ and column norm $CL$. Its first two fine differences have row norms $C/L^2,C/L^3$. All coarse slots have bounded row and column norms. At depth zero one may take $\ell_0=\sqrt{1-c_0}I$.
  - Explanation: The free precision admits a positive completion by localized edge operators, together with intertwining operators that resolve the identity and carry the fine-output gain in the blocking factor.
- **claim-rg-exact-step** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-rg-closure)): The constants can be chosen in the order described below so that integration by <a href="#rg:observation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:observation">[rg:observation]</a>, from an input with $b\in[H_j/2,2H_j]$, gives an exact representation <a href="#rg:density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:density">[rg:density]</a> on layer $j-1$, with free history $h+1$, all the renewed norm bounds, and $$b'=b+\alpha_h(\mathbf P)+\frac{\kappa_h(\mathbf P)}b+\Delta, \qquad \|\alpha_h\|+\|\kappa_h\|\le C_L, \qquad \|\Delta\|\le C_LH_j^{-1.05}. \label{rg:coupling-step}$$ The canonical map and $\alpha_h,\kappa_h$ depend only on the input canonical vector $\mathbf P$ and the free history. They do not depend on the precise coupling, the cutoffs, the errors, the covering gas, or the period. The output is iterable if its coupling belongs to the next admitted band; this additional fact is proved for the trajectories used below.* *For two inputs on common label lists and the same reference layer, let $\lambda=b_2-b_1$. Let $u$ be a fixed positively weighted sum of the seven coefficient discrepancies, the error discrepancy $\delta_j^{-1}\\|f_2-f_1\\|_{k-1,j,A}$, and the gas discrepancy $w_j^{-1}\\|k_2-k_1\\|_{j,A}$. The weights are independent of $j$ and the periods. The outputs have common compatible lists and satisfy $$\begin{aligned} u'&\le q u+C_LH_j^C\epsilon_h+C_LH_j^{-b_*}\|\lambda\|, \label{rg:shape-comparison}\\ \|\lambda'-\lambda\| &\le C_Lu+C_LH_j^C\epsilon_h+C_LH_j^{-b_*}\|\lambda\|, \label{rg:coupling-comparison} \end{aligned}$$ where $L^{-1}<q<1$, $b_*>0$, and $\epsilon_h=L^{-\min(h_1,h_2)}$ for unequal free histories (zero for equal histories). These estimates include bulk and all admitted periods.
  - Explanation: One exact block integration maps densities of the retained class to the same class one layer down, updating the kinetic coupling by a canonical two-loop drift plus a small error, with matching comparison estimates for two inputs.
- **claim-core-reserve** (bound, [source](#stmt-rg-core-reserve)): On the retained sector tests around a component with $s_c$ seeds, using these principal coordinates, $$\sum_{\rm assigned}(\mathcal F+\mathcal B+\mathcal C+ \mathcal U+\mathcal N+\mathcal Z) -\frac12\sum_{\rm selected,assigned}\|D\xi+F\|^2 \ge c t^2s_c. \label{rg:reserve}$$ The same estimate holds on the no-output-flag extension of a component with no output seeds. After extending the Gaussian coordinates beyond the original chart, it holds on the attached protector supports, including the supports of their fixed-order derivatives and of the retained sector-profile derivatives. No estimate for an arbitrary exponential preimage outside those supports is asserted.
  - Explanation: Around each component of rough seeds the retained energy rows leave a positive reserve proportional to the number of seeds after subtracting the selected affine squares.
- **claim-joint-prepared-factors** (bound, [source](#stmt-rg-joint)): The prepared factors have nonnegative majorants $a_\alpha$ such that, for every admissible finite selection $A$ in a fixed primary pattern, including its compulsory component factors, and every allowed normalized derivative of total order $r\le k$, $$\left\|D^r\mathbb E\prod_{\alpha\in A}F_\alpha\right\| \le C_k\left(1+\sum_{\alpha\in A}s_\alpha\right)^{d_k} \prod_{\alpha\in A}a_\alpha. \label{rg:joint-product}$$ For nonempty output inventory only the undifferentiated and parameter comparison forms are asserted. A single marked discrepancy retains its own size in the corresponding product bound. The load polynomial is absorbed by a reserved amount of support exponent. The product estimate is proved explicitly in Appendix <a href="#supp:joint-majorants" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="supp:joint-majorants">15</a>, with the concrete eight-class verification at its end. The majorants have the following summed bounds. An ordinary primitive of order $r>0$ has a summed majorant $C_Lg^r\operatorname{poly}(M,p)$, with any fixed required support exponent. The old errors retain their actual cap $\delta_j$. Exceptional primitives retain an arbitrary fixed power of $g$, and an output-seeded connected component retains a bound stronger than $e^{-p^{3/10}}$.* *For a component with empty output inventory, the same statements hold through $k$ normalized field derivatives on its no-output-flag extension, and through $k-1$ derivatives for a single input or parameter discrepancy. For a component with nonempty output inventory, only sup bounds and coupling/history differences at fixed $V$ and fixed output statuses are asserted. All discrepancies retain their own input difference or the factor $\epsilon_h+\lambda_g$, where $\lambda_g=\|b_2-b_1\|/H_j$.
  - Explanation: Prepared factors have nonnegative majorants whose product bounds hold jointly, including normalized derivatives of fixed order, under one common Gaussian law.
- **claim-canonical-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rg-canonical-contraction)): The list <a href="#rg:slots" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:slots">[rg:slots]</a> contains every nonscalar canonical term through total order four. In that order the map <a href="#rg:canonical-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:canonical-map">[rg:canonical-map]</a> is triangular, with same-slot linear response at most $C/L$ in the coefficient norm, where $C$ is fixed before $L$. All other responses are bounded by constants depending on $L$ and the preceding caps. The kick $\alpha$ does not depend on old $I_2$, and $\kappa$ does not depend on old $J_2$ at their respective orders.* *Consequently the caps in <a href="#rg:canonical-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:canonical-norm">[rg:canonical-norm]</a> can be chosen successively so that the canonical orbit starting from zero remains bounded. This orbit, and its kicks $\alpha_h,\kappa_h$, approach limits exponentially in $h$.
  - Explanation: The canonical slots exhaust all nonscalar terms through total order four, the induced map is triangular with diagonal response of order inverse blocking factor, and the canonical orbit with its kicks converges exponentially.
- **claim-error-contraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-rg-error-contraction)): For each fixed required output exponent $B$, and sufficiently large $L$ followed by $H$, $$\\|\mathcal T f\\|_{k,j-1,B} \le (C/L+o(1))\\|f\\|_{k,j,A}. \label{rg:error-contraction-bound}$$ The same bound applies to an input difference through order $k-1$. A change of map acting on an unchanged input instead costs $C_L\operatorname{poly}(M,p)\delta_j(\epsilon_h+\lambda_g)$ through order $k-1$.
  - Explanation: Isolated old regular errors contract in the weighted derivative norm under one block integration, with an inverse blocking factor gain plus a vanishing correction.
- **claim-calibration-kicks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-calibration)): For the nearest-neighbor O(4) normalization of <a href="#eq:measure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:measure">[eq:measure]</a>, and $\gamma=(\log L)/\pi$, $$\begin{aligned} B_s&=-\gamma s+O_L(1),& C_s&=-\frac{\gamma}{2\pi}s+O_L(1),\label{cal:sums}\\ \alpha_h&=-\gamma+O_L(\rho^h),& \kappa_h&=-\frac{\gamma}{2\pi}+O_L(\rho^h) \label{cal:kicks} \end{aligned}$$ for some $\rho<1$. The constants are independent of the number of subsequent block transformations.
  - Explanation: The canonical kinetic kicks converge exponentially to explicit constants fixed by the first two renormalization coefficients, so cumulative sums grow linearly with bounded corrections.
- **claim-depth-calibration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-calibration)): For every admitted nearest-neighbor trajectory of length $N$, starting with zero canonical interactions, whose terminal coupling belongs to a fixed compact interval $I_H$ about a sufficiently large $H$, its initial coupling $\beta$ satisfies $$\label{eq:calibration} N\log L=\pi\beta-\tfrac12\log\beta+O_{L,H}(1).$$ In particular $L^{-N}$ is bounded above and below by positive fixed multiples of $\sqrt\beta e^{-\pi\beta}$.
  - Explanation: For admitted trajectories ending in the terminal interval, the blocking depth is determined by the initial coupling up to a bounded error including the logarithmic correction, which fixes the cutoff scale.
- **claim-admission-shooting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rg-admission)): Fix $L,P_0,H$ as above, increasing $H$ if necessary, and put $I_H=[.9H,1.1H]$. Every sufficiently large prescribed bare coupling $b$ has a depth $N$ for which the nearest-neighbor trajectory remains in all its admitted bands and ends at a coupling in $I_H$. Conversely, for every $N\ge1$ and every $h_0\in I_H$ there is a bare coupling $b_N(h_0)$ whose admitted trajectory of depth $N$ ends exactly at $h_0$.* *For each of these trajectories, uniformly in its depth and terminal value, $$N\log L=\pi b-\tfrac12\log b+O_{L,H}(1), \qquad L^N\asymp_{L,H} b^{-1/2}e^{\pi b}. \label{rg:scale-calibration}$$ No uniqueness of the shooting value is asserted or needed.
  - Explanation: Every large bare coupling has an admitted trajectory ending in the terminal interval, and every terminal value in that interval is reached exactly by some bare coupling at each depth.
- **claim-matched-endpoint** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-matched-endpoint)): Let $N\ge K\ge1$, and choose any two admitted nearest-neighbor trajectories of these depths with the same terminal coupling $h_0\in I_H$. On every common admitted terminal period $m$, write their exact endpoint densities as $$\rho_r(V)=e^{v_rm^2}e^{X_r(V)}\Xi_r(V),\qquad r=N,K,$$ using common compatible label lists. The numbers $v_r$ are independent of $m$. There are constants $C_H,d_H>0$, independent of $N,K,h_0,m$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \\|X_N-X_K\\|_\infty &\le C_Hm^2e^{-d_HK},\nonumber\\ \sup_x\sum_{\ell:x\in P_\ell} e^{As_\ell}\\|k_{N,\ell}-k_{K,\ell}\\|_\infty &\le C_He^{-d_HK}. \label{rg:terminal-difference} \end{aligned}$$ All the endpoint class bounds hold uniformly. In particular, with $t=t_0$, the canonical part of $-X_r$ is bounded by $C_Lm^2(Ht^4+t^2)$ and its error part by $m^2H^{-2.05}$. Every covering support has a connected hull of at most $R_Hs_\ell$ sites. Changing a set $B$ of endpoint spins changes $X_r$ by at most $D_H\|B\|$. The constants $R_H,D_H$ can be chosen polynomial in $H$.
  - Explanation: Two admitted trajectories of different depths with the same terminal coupling have endpoint densities whose exponents and covering weights differ by an amount decaying exponentially in the shorter depth.
- **claim-relative-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-relative-comparison)): Suppose <span class="upright">(C1)–(C4)</span> hold and $\delta_K\le C_H e^{-\gamma_H K}$, where $\gamma_H>0$. Fix $0<\zeta_H<\gamma_H/2$. For every sufficiently large fixed $H$ there are constants $M_H,C_H,a_H>0$ such that, whenever $M\ge M_H$, there is a common measurable set $G=G_{H,K,M}$ satisfying $$\begin{aligned} \mu_i(G^c)&\le C_HM^2e^{-a_HK},\qquad i=1,2, \label{cmp:bad-probability}\\ \left\|\frac{\Xi_2(q)}{\Xi_1(q)}-1\right\| &\le \exp\!\left(C_HM^2\delta_K e^{\zeta_HK}\right)-1, \qquad q\in G. \label{cmp:relative-cover} \end{aligned}$$ The same estimate holds after interchanging $1$ and $2$. Thus $$\left\|\log\frac{\rho_2(q)}{\rho_1(q)}\right\| \le C_HM^2\delta_K e^{\zeta_HK},\qquad q\in G. \label{cmp:relative-density}$$ There are also $c_H,d_H>0$ and $K_H<\infty$ such that $$\left\|\log\frac{Z_2}{Z_1}\right\|\le C_He^{-d_HK} \quad\text{if}\quad K\ge K_H,\qquad M_H\le M,\qquad M^2\le e^{c_HK}. \label{cmp:partition-conclusion}$$
  - Explanation: On a common set of high probability under both laws the complete densities agree in relative terms, so the two partition functions are exponentially close for volumes that are not too large.
- **claim-joint-rarity** (bound, [source](#stmt-cmp-rarity)): For sufficiently large $H$, there is $\sigma_H=cHt_H^2$, with $c>0$ fixed after $C_1$, such that $$\mu_i\{J\subset\widehat D\}\le e^{-\sigma_H\|J\|} \label{cmp:joint-rarity}$$ for every set $J$ of distinct bonds and $i=1,2$.
  - Explanation: Any prescribed set of distinct flagged bonds has probability decaying exponentially in its cardinality under each positive law, by reflection positivity and chessboard estimates.
- **claim-quantitative-filling** (bound, [source](#stmt-cmp-filling)): An $L_0$-Lipschitz map from the boundary of the unit square to $S^3$ has an extension to the square with Lipschitz constant at most $C(1+L_0)^{3/2}$. The extension can be chosen measurably as a function of the boundary map.
  - Explanation: A Lipschitz map from the boundary of a square into the three-sphere extends inside with Lipschitz constant bounded by a power of the boundary constant, chosen measurably.
- **claim-disjoint-editable-regions** (construction, [source](#stmt-cmp-editing)): For fixed sufficiently large constants $C_2,C_1$, in that order, and $M\ge M_H$, each configuration has a finite family of disjoint editable site sets $E_C$, with nonempty fixed inventories $D_C\subset D(q)$, such that $$D(q)=\bigsqcup_C D_C.$$ If $n_C$ is the number of original flagged bonds assigned to this region, then $n_C\ge1$, the assigned flagged sets are disjoint, and $$\|E_C\|\le R_{E,H}n_C,\qquad E_C\text{ has a connected hull of at most }R_{E,H}n_C\text{ sites}, \qquad R_{E,H}\le Ct_H^{-2}. \label{cmp:edit-size}$$ One can replace the spins in any selected $E_C$, leaving all other spins and the region’s outer boundary unchanged, so that every true rough bond of $D_C$ disappears and no true rough bond is created. All choices can be made measurably.
  - Explanation: Each configuration has disjoint editable regions of size controlled by the number of flagged bonds they hold, inside which spins can be replaced to remove all rough bonds without creating new ones.
- **claim-removal-inclusion-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-removal-identities)): The background left after selecting ${\cal A}$ has total weight $$\Xi_i(I\setminus B_{\cal A};S_{\cal A}). \label{cmp:background-identity}$$ Consequently $$\begin{aligned} \Xi_i(I;S) &= \sum_{\substack{{\cal A}\ {\rm compatible}\\ S_U\cap S\ne\varnothing\ \forall U\in{\cal A}}} (-1)^{\|{\cal A}\|} \left[\prod_{U\in{\cal A}}w_i(U;q(I))\right] \Xi_i(I\setminus B_{\cal A};S_{\cal A}), \label{cmp:IE}\\ \Xi_2(I)-\Xi_1(I) &= \sum_{\substack{{\cal A}\ {\rm compatible}\\{\cal A}\ne\varnothing}} \left[\prod_{U\in{\cal A}} \bigl(w_2(U;q(I))-w_1(U;q(I))\bigr)\right] \Xi_1(I\setminus B_{\cal A};S_{\cal A}). \label{cmp:difference-identity} \end{aligned}$$ The empty term in <a href="#cmp:IE" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cmp:IE">[cmp:IE]</a> is $\Xi_i(I)$.
  - Explanation: Removing selected macrolabels leaves a background covering sum evaluated on the reduced active set, which gives exact inclusion-exclusion identities for the signed covering sum and for the difference of two such sums.
- **claim-exceptional-set-probability** (bound, [source](#stmt-cmp-exceptional)): For every polynomial choice of $C_{4,H}$, sufficiently large $H$ admits an $a_H>0$ such that $$\mu_i(G^c)\le2v e^{-a_HK},\qquad i=1,2 . \label{cmp:exceptional-bound}$$
  - Explanation: The set of configurations violating the geometric balance condition for connected collections has exponentially small probability under both laws, by a flag-counting entropy bound and joint rarity.
- **claim-forest-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-cmp-forest)): For a sufficiently large polynomial $C_{4,H}$ and sufficiently large $H$, the set $G$ above satisfies $$\frac{\|\Xi_2(q)-\Xi_1(q)\|}{\Xi_1(q)} \le \exp(D_Hv\delta_K e^{\zeta_HK})-1 . \label{cmp:forest-conclusion}$$ The same estimate holds with denominator $\Xi_2(q)$.
  - Explanation: On the good set the normalized difference of the two covering sums is bounded by an exponential of a small quantity, using an exact encoding of the signed recursion by rooted forests.
- **claim-normalization-common-set** (bound, [source](#stmt-cmp-normalization)): Let $f_1,f_2>0$ be integrable, with $0<Z_i=\int f_i\,\,\mathrm d\nu<\infty$ and $\pi_i=f_i\nu/Z_i$. If $$\pi_i(G^c)\le\varepsilon_i<1,\qquad \left\|\log\frac{f_2}{f_1}\right\|\le\eta\quad\hbox{on }G,$$ then $$e^{-\eta}(1-\varepsilon_1) \le\frac{Z_2}{Z_1} \le\frac{e^\eta}{1-\varepsilon_2}. \label{cmp:normalization-bound}$$ If $\varepsilon_i\le\varepsilon\le1/2$, then $\left\|\log(Z_2/Z_1)\right\|\le\eta+2\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: A uniform relative density bound on a set of high probability under both normalized laws gives a two-sided bound on the ratio of normalizing constants.
- **claim-cutoff-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cutoff)): There are constants $C_H,c_H,d_H>0$ and a finite depth threshold such that, for all admitted $N\ge K$, $h\in I_H$, and dyadic $M\ge M_{\min}(L,H)$ with $(2M)^2\le e^{c_HK}$, $$\label{eq:cutoff-comparison} \|D_N(h;M)-D_K(h;M)\|\le C_He^{-d_HK},\qquad D_r(h;M)=\Delta_{\beta_r(h)}(ML^r).$$ The constants do not depend on the ratio of the two bare couplings or on $N-K$.
  - Explanation: For admitted depths at least a reference depth and a coarse box of moderate volume, the square-box defects at the two cutoffs differ by an amount exponentially small in the reference depth.
- **claim-lower-mass-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-lower)): There are fixed $K_0,M_0$, independent of the final depth, such that $$\label{eq:lower-depth} m_{\mathrm{lat}}(\beta_N(h))\ge\frac{\log2}{M_0L^N}, \qquad N\ge K_0,\quad h\in I_H.$$ In particular $m_{\mathrm{lat}}(\beta)\ge c\sqrt\beta e^{-\pi\beta}$ for every sufficiently large bare coupling, with $c>0$.
  - Explanation: One reference depth and one coarse box, fixed independently of the final depth, make the square-box defect small at every finer admitted cutoff, giving a lower gap bound proportional to the inverse physical side.
- **claim-terminal-alignment** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-alignment)): For the actual retained densities of Theorem <a href="#thm:rg-closure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rg-closure">9</a>, uniformly in the cutoff depth and the admitted terminal coupling, $$\label{cmp:alignment} \mathbb E\|V_X-V_{X+e}\|^2\le\varepsilon_H,\qquad \varepsilon_H=(t_H/C_1)^2+4e^{-\sigma_H} \longrightarrow0\quad(H\to\infty).$$ If $H$ is large enough that $\varepsilon_H\le1/9$, then $$\label{cmp:block-correlation} \mathbb E[V_0^{(1)}V_{3e}^{(1)}]\ge\frac18.$$ At depth $N$ this is the correlation of two centered original-spin observables of norm at most one, supported in their respective ancestral blocks of side $L^N$.
  - Explanation: Neighboring retained spins are aligned on average uniformly in depth and terminal coupling, which yields a positive correlation of one spin component at a short fixed separation, pulled back to block observables of the original spins.
- **claim-upper-mass-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-upper)): For all the admitted trajectories used above, $$\label{eq:upper-depth} m_{\mathrm{lat}}(\beta_N(h))\le(\log8)L^{-N}.$$
  - Explanation: The nonvanishing block correlation across a support gap bounds the full gap above by a constant multiple of the inverse block side.
- **claim-continuum-spectrum** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-continuum)): Let $a_j\downarrow0$ and let $T_j$ be positive lattice transfer contractions with normalized vacua $\Omega_j$ and full gaps $m_j$ satisfying $\liminf_jm_j/a_j\ge m_*>0$. Suppose a limiting theory has Hilbert space $\mathcal H$, normalized vacuum $\Omega$, and a strongly continuous semigroup $e^{-t\mathsf H}$ with $\mathsf H\ge0$ self-adjoint and $\mathsf H\Omega=0$. Suppose a set of centered vectors $u$ dense in $\Omega^\perp$ has centered lattice approximants $u_j\perp\Omega_j$ and nonnegative integers $n_j(t)$ such that $$\left\lVert u_j\right\rVert^2\to\left\lVert u\right\rVert^2,\qquad \langle u_j,T_j^{n_j(t)}u_j\rangle \to\langle u,e^{-t\mathsf H}u\rangle, \qquad a_jn_j(t)\to t$$ for every $t\ge0$. Then $$\operatorname{spec}(\mathsf H)\subset\{0\}\cup[m_*,\infty),\qquad \ker\mathsf H=\mathbb C\Omega.$$
  - Explanation: If lattice transfer contractions have gaps bounded below in physical units and the correlation functions of approximating vectors converge to those of a limiting semigroup, the limiting generator has a spectral gap at least that bound.
- **claim-free-strip-uniform** (bound, [source](#stmt-app-free-strip)): For the sampled smooth block weights in Section <a href="#free:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="free:section">4</a>, there are $\delta,c,C>0$ and $L_0$ with the following properties for $L\ge L_0$ and $h\ge0$. The precisions $K_h$ and an elliptic edge lift $B_h$ are analytic on the complex $\delta$-neighborhood of the momentum torus. On the real torus, $$cD\le K_h\le CD,\qquad cI\le B_h\le CI, \qquad K_h=d^*B_hd,\quad B_h(0)=I.$$ Their fixed derivatives are bounded, $K_h-D$ has a zero of order four at zero, and their history differences, including the inverse edge lifts, are bounded by $CL^{-h}$ when compared with any depth $\bar h\ge h$. The same assertions hold on a common smaller strip.
  - Explanation: Free precisions and elliptic edge lifts are analytic and uniformly bounded on a fixed complex neighborhood of the momentum torus, independently of depth, with history differences decaying in the depth.
- **claim-alias-division** (construction, [source](#stmt-app-alias-division)): Suppose an analytic alias vector $R_l(p)$ satisfies $d(k_l)^*R_l(p)=0$, has the bounds $$\|R_l(p)\|\le C_sL^{-1}\langle l\rangle^{-s}$$ on a fixed larger domain for every required fixed $s$, and has a zero through first order at the principal origin. Then $R_l=c(k_l)^*\gamma_l$ on a smaller fixed domain, with $\|\gamma_l\|\le C_s\langle l\rangle^{-s}$ and $\gamma_0(0)=0$. If $R_l(0)=0$ on every nonprincipal alias, then $\gamma_l(0)=0$ for all $l$.* *If, in addition, $\gamma_l(0)=0$ for every alias, there is an analytic row $H_l$ satisfying $H_ld'(p)=\gamma_l(p)$ and $\|H_l\|\le C_s\langle l\rangle^{-s}$. Both constructions are linear, preserve alias gluing, and have constants independent of $L$.
  - Explanation: A divergence-free analytic alias vector vanishing to first order at the origin factors through the curl operator and then through the coarse differences, with decay in the alias index and constants independent of the blocking factor.
- **claim-one-norm-contraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-supp-one-norm-contraction)): Fix $0<\sigma\le c_*/2$. Under <a href="#supp:fixed-norm-kernel-assumption" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="supp:fixed-norm-kernel-assumption">[supp:fixed-norm-kernel-assumption]</a>, substitution <a href="#supp:fixed-norm-substitution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="supp:fixed-norm-substitution">[supp:fixed-norm-substitution]</a> has the following bounds in the single norm just defined: $$\\|A'_n\\|_\sigma\le C L^{2-n}\\|A_n\\|_\sigma \quad(3\le n\le6),\qquad \\|A'_2\\|_\sigma\le \frac C L\\|A_2\\|_\sigma. \label{supp:one-norm-conclusion}$$ The quadratic assertion is for sums of compensated packets and includes the cost of output compensation. To regard this compensation as a representation of the same polynomial, assume additionally <a href="#supp:fixed-norm-affine-assumption" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="supp:fixed-norm-affine-assumption">[supp:fixed-norm-affine-assumption]</a> and that the output polynomial at each coarse anchor is invariant under the square symmetries. The latter holds for bulk translation-invariant input coefficients and a block-centered, square-equivariant kernel $P$. In this case the entire transferred quadratic polynomial has zero affine Hessian, and its stiffness extraction is exactly zero.* *The constant $C$ and a lower threshold for $L$ depend only on $\sigma$, $c_*$, $K_1$, $K_2$, and the fixed tensor conventions. They do not depend on $L$, the history, or the coefficient lists. One can consequently choose $L$ after $C$ so that every bound in <a href="#supp:one-norm-conclusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="supp:one-norm-conclusion">[supp:one-norm-conclusion]</a> is a strict contraction.
  - Explanation: Substituting the linear conditional mean into canonical coefficient lists contracts a single exponential path norm for large blocking factor, including the cost of output compensation of quadratic packets.
- **claim-principal-log** (bound, [source](#stmt-app-principal-log)): Suppose that all internal edge defaults of an $L$-block and its observation default are retained in a product. On the support of this product, or of any product-rule term of a fixed-order derivative of its smooth profiles, every constituent spin satisfies $$\|q_xV_Y^{-1}-1\|\le K_Lt.$$ If $q_x=\exp(\xi_x)V_Y$ and $\|\xi_x\|<\pi$, then $\xi_x$ is the principal logarithm and $\|\xi_x\|\le 2K_Lt$ for sufficiently large $H$.
  - Explanation: When the block defaults are retained, all constituent spins are close to the block reference, so the exponential chart coordinate is the principal logarithm and is small.
- **claim-local-marginals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-app-local-marginals)): The covariance $C_P(x,y)$ vanishes for $d(x,y)>R$. For $I\subset E(P)$ its marginal matrix $C_P[I,I]$ depends only on $P\cap N_{R+3M}(I)$. Its numerical mean on $I$ also depends only on the mean windows, affine-row field arguments, and hole queries specified above.* *Suppose finitely many prepared factors have disjoint complete supports. Their Gaussian coordinate sets have distance greater than $R$, their frozen-spin and tag argument sets are disjoint, and their integrals factor exactly. Each individual integral is unchanged on deleting outside primary components whose complete supports are disjoint from its own, with all recorded external fields and status answers fixed.
  - Explanation: The window covariance has finite range, its marginals depend only on nearby seeds, and prepared factors with disjoint complete supports have integrals that factor exactly.
- **claim-gaussian-product** (bound, [source](#stmt-supp-gaussian-product)): Let $Z$ be a centered Gaussian vector with covariance $C$, where $0\le C\le c_*I$ and $c_*>0$. Let $O_i$ satisfy $\|O_i\|\le a_i$. For a finite collection of ratio factors put $$R_\alpha=e^{Q_\alpha}-1,\qquad \|Q_\alpha(Z)\|\le e_\alpha \left(1+\sum_{x\in I_\alpha}\|Z_x\|^2\right), \qquad 0<e_\alpha\le1.$$ Suppose that $$\begin{aligned} \sup_x\sum_{\alpha:x\in I_\alpha}\sqrt{e_\alpha} &\le(64c_*)^{-1},\label{supp:endpoint-load}\\ (2+4c_*\|I_\alpha\|)\sqrt{e_\alpha} &\le\tfrac14\log(e_\alpha^{-1}) \quad\hbox{for every }\alpha.\label{supp:ratio-size} \end{aligned}$$ Let $F_1,\ldots,F_n$ be bounded factors whose product has absolute value at most $\mathbf1_E$, where $\mathbb P(E)\le e^{-\tau n}$. Finally suppose $\\|S\\|_{L^4}\le B$. Then $$\mathbb E\left\|S\prod_iO_i\prod_\alpha R_\alpha \prod_{j=1}^nF_j\right\| \le B\prod_i a_i\prod_\alpha e_\alpha^{1/4} e^{-\tau n/2}.$$ The convention for $n=0$ is $E$ equal to the full probability space. The conclusion also holds for factors $R_\alpha$ satisfying directly $\|R_\alpha\|\le\sqrt{e_\alpha}\exp\{2\sqrt{e_\alpha} (1+\sum_{x\in I_\alpha}\|Z_x\|^2)\}$. Here $x$ indexes scalar Gaussian coordinates; vector coordinates may be replaced by their scalar components. No independence between any of the displayed factors is required.
  - Explanation: The expectation of a product of deterministic envelopes, ratio factors and a simultaneous failure indicator under a bounded-covariance Gaussian is bounded by the product of envelopes without any independence assumption.
- **claim-product-reserve** (bound, [source](#stmt-supp-product-reserve)): Let the factors in Lemma <a href="#supp:gaussian-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="supp:gaussian-product">38</a> carry integer loads $s\ge1$, and let $s_{\rm tot}$ be their total load. Fix an integer $k$. Suppose that each differentiated product, through order $k$, is a sum of at most $C_k(1+s_{\rm tot})^{d_k}$ expressions to which that lemma applies. Each expression retains the original factor indices and envelopes $a_i,e_\alpha,I_\alpha$, the same failure count $n$, and the same core loads; differentiated factors may use the direct ratio envelope stated in that lemma. Suppose, for every such expression, $$B\le C_k g^{-d_k}(1+s_{\rm tot})^{d_k},\qquad 0<g<1.$$ Suppose each expression requiring the factor $g^{-d_k}$ has at least one of the following reserves:* 1. *a deterministic core factor bounded by $e^{-\kappa p^2s_c}$, with $\kappa p^2/2\ge d_k\log(g^{-1})$;* 2. *at least one failure test, with $\tau/4\ge d_k\log(g^{-1})$;* 3. *at least one ratio factor, with $\log(e_\alpha^{-1})/8\ge d_k\log(g^{-1})$;* 4. *a deterministic exceptional factor with envelope $a_*\le e_*$, where $0<e_*<1$ and $\log(e_*^{-1})/2\ge d_k\log(g^{-1})$.* *Expressions without any such factor are assumed to obey the same bound on $B$ without $g^{-d_k}$. Then the derivative has a joint product majorant with deterministic ordinary weights $a_i$, core weights $e^{-\kappa p^2s_c/2}$, failure weights $e^{-\tau/4}$, and ratio weights $e_\alpha^{1/8}$, and deterministic exceptional weights $e_*^{1/2}$ replacing their original envelopes, times a fixed polynomial in $1+s_{\rm tot}$. For a single marked parameter or input discrepancy, factor out its size and use an envelope for the remaining marked factor in place of its original envelope. No bound relative to the original factor is required; that factor may vanish while its discrepancy does not. If the hypotheses above, including the reserves, hold with this replacement, and all other envelopes hold uniformly along the comparison or interpolation, the same conclusion retains the discrepancy size.
  - Explanation: Fixed-order derivatives of such products keep a joint product majorant: polynomial and inverse-coupling losses are paid by a core, failure, ratio or exceptional reserve.

```json
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-stack",
        "exact_quote": "One can choose a common $c_0>0$ and edge operators $\\ell_h$ such that $$\\mathsf A_h=\\binom{\\sqrt{c_0}I}{\\ell_h},\\qquad X_h=\\mathsf A_hd,$$ give the free precision $X_h^*X_h=K_h$. There are real operators $\\mathcal M_h,\\mathcal N_h$ satisfying $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal M_h^*\\mathcal M_h+\\mathcal N_h^*\\mathcal N_h&=I,\n \\label{free:isometry}\\\\\n \\mathcal M_h^*(X_hu,\\sqrt a(V-Qu))&=X_{h+1}V,\n \\label{free:ambient}\\\\\n \\mathcal M_hX_{h+1}&=(X_hP,\\sqrt a(I-QP)),\n \\label{free:harmonic}\\\\\n \\mathcal N_hX_{h+1}&=0.\n \\label{free:null}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All kernels have exponential moments and the history bounds of Lemma <a href=\"#free:bounds\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"free:bounds\">7</a>. The fine-output part of $\\mathcal M_h$ has weighted row norm $C/L$ and column norm $CL$. Its first two fine differences have row norms $C/L^2,C/L^3$. All coarse slots have bounded row and column norms. At depth zero one may take $\\ell_0=\\sqrt{1-c_0}I$.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-rg-closure",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"rg:closure\" label=\"rg:closure\"></span>The constants can be chosen in the order described below so that integration by <a href=\"#rg:observation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rg:observation\">[rg:observation]</a>, from an input with $b\\in[H_j/2,2H_j]$, gives an exact representation <a href=\"#rg:density\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rg:density\">[rg:density]</a> on layer $j-1$, with free history $h+1$, all the renewed norm bounds, and $$b'=b+\\alpha_h(\\mathbf P)+\\frac{\\kappa_h(\\mathbf P)}b+\\Delta,\n \\qquad |\\alpha_h|+|\\kappa_h|\\le C_L,\n \\qquad |\\Delta|\\le C_LH_j^{-1.05}.\n \\label{rg:coupling-step}$$ The canonical map and $\\alpha_h,\\kappa_h$ depend only on the input canonical vector $\\mathbf P$ and the free history. They do not depend on the precise coupling, the cutoffs, the errors, the covering gas, or the period. The output is iterable if its coupling belongs to the next admitted band; this additional fact is proved for the trajectories used below.*\n\n\n\n*For two inputs on common label lists and the same reference layer, let $\\lambda=b_2-b_1$. Let $u$ be a fixed positively weighted sum of the seven coefficient discrepancies, the error discrepancy $\\delta_j^{-1}\\|f_2-f_1\\|_{k-1,j,A}$, and the gas discrepancy $w_j^{-1}\\|k_2-k_1\\|_{j,A}$. The weights are independent of $j$ and the periods. The outputs have common compatible lists and satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n u'&\\le q u+C_LH_j^C\\epsilon_h+C_LH_j^{-b_*}|\\lambda|,\n \\label{rg:shape-comparison}\\\\\n |\\lambda'-\\lambda|\n    &\\le C_Lu+C_LH_j^C\\epsilon_h+C_LH_j^{-b_*}|\\lambda|,\n \\label{rg:coupling-comparison}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $L^{-1}<q<1$, $b_*>0$, and $\\epsilon_h=L^{-\\min(h_1,h_2)}$ for unequal free histories (zero for equal histories). These estimates include bulk and all admitted periods.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-rg-core-reserve",
        "exact_quote": "On the retained sector tests around a component with $s_c$ seeds, using these principal coordinates, $$\\sum_{\\rm assigned}(\\mathcal F+\\mathcal B+\\mathcal C+\n                           \\mathcal U+\\mathcal N+\\mathcal Z)\n -\\frac12\\sum_{\\rm selected,assigned}|D\\xi+F|^2\n                   \\ge c t^2s_c.\n \\label{rg:reserve}$$ The same estimate holds on the no-output-flag extension of a component with no output seeds. After extending the Gaussian coordinates beyond the original chart, it holds on the attached protector supports, including the supports of their fixed-order derivatives and of the retained sector-profile derivatives. No estimate for an arbitrary exponential preimage outside those supports is asserted.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rg-joint",
        "exact_quote": "The prepared factors have nonnegative majorants $a_\\alpha$ such that, for every admissible finite selection $A$ in a fixed primary pattern, including its compulsory component factors, and every allowed normalized derivative of total order $r\\le k$, $$\\left|D^r\\mathbb E\\prod_{\\alpha\\in A}F_\\alpha\\right|\n \\le C_k\\left(1+\\sum_{\\alpha\\in A}s_\\alpha\\right)^{d_k}\n                      \\prod_{\\alpha\\in A}a_\\alpha.\n \\label{rg:joint-product}$$ For nonempty output inventory only the undifferentiated and parameter comparison forms are asserted. A single marked discrepancy retains its own size in the corresponding product bound. The load polynomial is absorbed by a reserved amount of support exponent. The product estimate is proved explicitly in Appendix <a href=\"#supp:joint-majorants\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"supp:joint-majorants\">15</a>, with the concrete eight-class verification at its end. The majorants have the following summed bounds. An ordinary primitive of order $r>0$ has a summed majorant $C_Lg^r\\operatorname{poly}(M,p)$, with any fixed required support exponent. The old errors retain their actual cap $\\delta_j$. Exceptional primitives retain an arbitrary fixed power of $g$, and an output-seeded connected component retains a bound stronger than $e^{-p^{3/10}}$.*\n\n*For a component with empty output inventory, the same statements hold through $k$ normalized field derivatives on its no-output-flag extension, and through $k-1$ derivatives for a single input or parameter discrepancy. For a component with nonempty output inventory, only sup bounds and coupling/history differences at fixed $V$ and fixed output statuses are asserted. All discrepancies retain their own input difference or the factor $\\epsilon_h+\\lambda_g$, where $\\lambda_g=|b_2-b_1|/H_j$.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rg-canonical-contraction",
        "exact_quote": "The list <a href=\"#rg:slots\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rg:slots\">[rg:slots]</a> contains every nonscalar canonical term through total order four. In that order the map <a href=\"#rg:canonical-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rg:canonical-map\">[rg:canonical-map]</a> is triangular, with same-slot linear response at most $C/L$ in the coefficient norm, where $C$ is fixed before $L$. All other responses are bounded by constants depending on $L$ and the preceding caps. The kick $\\alpha$ does not depend on old $I_2$, and $\\kappa$ does not depend on old $J_2$ at their respective orders.*\n\n*Consequently the caps in <a href=\"#rg:canonical-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rg:canonical-norm\">[rg:canonical-norm]</a> can be chosen successively so that the canonical orbit starting from zero remains bounded. This orbit, and its kicks $\\alpha_h,\\kappa_h$, approach limits exponentially in $h$.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-calibration",
        "exact_quote": "For every admitted nearest-neighbor trajectory of length $N$, starting with zero canonical interactions, whose terminal coupling belongs to a fixed compact interval $I_H$ about a sufficiently large $H$, its initial coupling $\\beta$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:calibration}\n N\\log L=\\pi\\beta-\\tfrac12\\log\\beta+O_{L,H}(1).$$ In particular $L^{-N}$ is bounded above and below by positive fixed multiples of $\\sqrt\\beta e^{-\\pi\\beta}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-calibration",
          "exact_quote": "For every admitted nearest-neighbor trajectory of length $N$, starting with zero canonical interactions, whose terminal coupling belongs to a fixed compact interval $I_H$ about a sufficiently large $H$, its initial coupling $\\beta$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:calibration}\n N\\log L=\\pi\\beta-\\tfrac12\\log\\beta+O_{L,H}(1).$$ In particular $L^{-N}$ is bounded above and below by positive fixed multiples of $\\sqrt\\beta e^{-\\pi\\beta}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-215.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every admitted nearest-neighbor trajectory of length $N$, starting with zero canonical interactions, whose terminal coupling belongs to a fixed compact interval $I_H$ about a sufficiently large $H$, its initial coupling $\\beta$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:calibration}\n N\\log L=\\pi\\beta-\\tfrac12\\log\\beta+O_{L,H}(1).$$ In particular $L^{-N}$ is bounded above and below by positive fixed multiples of $\\sqrt\\beta e^{-\\pi\\beta}$.",
      "explanation": "For admitted trajectories ending in the terminal interval, the blocking depth is determined by the initial coupling up to a bounded error including the logarithmic correction, which fixes the cutoff scale.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rg-admission",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $L,P_0,H$ as above, increasing $H$ if necessary, and put $I_H=[.9H,1.1H]$. Every sufficiently large prescribed bare coupling $b$ has a depth $N$ for which the nearest-neighbor trajectory remains in all its admitted bands and ends at a coupling in $I_H$. Conversely, for every $N\\ge1$ and every $h_0\\in I_H$ there is a bare coupling $b_N(h_0)$ whose admitted trajectory of depth $N$ ends exactly at $h_0$.*\n\n\n*For each of these trajectories, uniformly in its depth and terminal value, $$N\\log L=\\pi b-\\tfrac12\\log b+O_{L,H}(1),\n \\qquad\n L^N\\asymp_{L,H} b^{-1/2}e^{\\pi b}.\n \\label{rg:scale-calibration}$$ No uniqueness of the shooting value is asserted or needed.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rg-admission",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $L,P_0,H$ as above, increasing $H$ if necessary, and put $I_H=[.9H,1.1H]$.           "source_id": "PM-215.4"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-matched-endpoint",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"rg:matching\" label=\"rg:matching\"></span>Let $N\\ge K\\ge1$, and choose any two admitted nearest-neighbor trajectories of these depths with the same terminal coupling $h_0\\in I_H$. On every common admitted terminal period $m$, write their exact endpoint densities as $$\\rho_r(V)=e^{v_rm^2}e^{X_r(V)}\\Xi_r(V),\\qquad r=N,K,$$ using common compatible label lists. The numbers $v_r$ are independent of $m$. There are constants $C_H,d_H>0$, independent of $N,K,h_0,m$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|X_N-X_K\\|_\\infty\n       &\\le C_Hm^2e^{-d_HK},\\nonumber\\\\\n \\sup_x\\sum_{\\ell:x\\in P_\\ell}\n      e^{As_\\ell}\\|k_{N,\\ell}-k_{K,\\ell}\\|_\\infty\n       &\\le C_He^{-d_HK}.\n \\label{rg:terminal-difference}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All the endpoint class bounds hold uniformly. In particular, with $t=t_0$, the canonical part of $-X_r$ is bounded by $C_Lm^2(Ht^4+t^2)$ and its error part by $m^2H^{-2.05}$. Every covering support has a connected hull of at most $R_Hs_\\ell$ sites. Changing a set $B$ of endpoint spins changes $X_r$ by at most $D_H|B|$. The constants $R_H,D_H$ can be chosen polynomial in $H$.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-matched-endpoint",

In particular, with $t=t_0$, the canonical part of $-X_r$ is bounded by $C_Lm^2(Ht^4+t^2)$ and its error part by $m^2H^{-2.05}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-relative-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose <span class=\"upright\">(C1)–(C4)</span> hold and $\\delta_K\\le C_H e^{-\\gamma_H K}$, where $\\gamma_H>0$. Fix $0<\\zeta_H<\\gamma_H/2$. For every sufficiently large fixed $H$ there are constants $M_H,C_H,a_H>0$ such that, whenever $M\\ge M_H$, there is a common measurable set $G=G_{H,K,M}$ satisfying $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mu_i(G^c)&\\le C_HM^2e^{-a_HK},\\qquad i=1,2,\n                    \\label{cmp:bad-probability}\\\\\n \\left|\\frac{\\Xi_2(q)}{\\Xi_1(q)}-1\\right|\n   &\\le \\exp\\!\\left(C_HM^2\\delta_K e^{\\zeta_HK}\\right)-1,\n                       \\qquad q\\in G. \\label{cmp:relative-cover}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same estimate holds after interchanging $1$ and $2$. Thus $$\\left|\\log\\frac{\\rho_2(q)}{\\rho_1(q)}\\right|\n       \\le C_HM^2\\delta_K e^{\\zeta_HK},\\qquad q\\in G.\n \\label{cmp:relative-density}$$ There are also $c_H,d_H>0$ and $K_H<\\infty$ such that $$\\left|\\log\\frac{Z_2}{Z_1}\\right|\\le C_He^{-d_HK}\n \\quad\\text{if}\\quad\n K\\ge K_H,\\qquad M_H\\le M,\\qquad M^2\\le e^{c_HK}.\n \\label{cmp:partition-conclusion}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-relative-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose <span class=\"upright\">(C1)–(C4)</span> hold and $\\delta_K\\le C_H e^{-\\gamma_H K}$, where $\\gamma_H>0$. Fix $0<\\zeta_H<\\gamma_H/2$. Thus $$\\left|\\log\\frac{\\rho_2(q)}{\\rho_1(q)}\\right|\n       \\le C_HM^2\\delta_K e^{\\zeta_HK},\\qquad q\\in G.\n \\label{cmp:relative-density}$$ There are also $c_H,d_H>0$ and $K_H<\\infty$ such that $$\\left|\\log\\frac{Z_2}{Z_1}\\right|\\le C_He^{-d_HK}\n \\quad\\text{if}\\quad\n K\\ge K_H,\\qquad M_H\\le M,\\qquad M^2\\le e^{c_HK}.\n \\label{cmp:partition-conclusion}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-215.4"

      "exact_statement": "Suppose <span class=\"upright\">(C1)–(C4)</span> hold and $\\delta_K\\le C_H e^{-\\gamma_H K}$, where $\\gamma_H>0$. Fix $0<\\zeta_H<\\gamma_H/2$. For every sufficiently large fixed $H$ there are constants $M_H,C_H,a_H>0$ such that, whenever $M\\ge M_H$, there is a common measurable set $G=G_{H,K,M}$ satisfying $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mu_i(G^c)&\\le C_HM^2e^{-a_HK},\\qquad i=1,2,\n                    \\label{cmp:bad-probability}\\\\\n \\left|\\frac{\\Xi_2(q)}{\\Xi_1(q)}-1\\right|\n   &\\le \\exp\\!\\left(C_HM^2\\delta_K e^{\\zeta_HK}\\right)-1,\n                       \\qquad q\\in G. \\label{cmp:relative-cover}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same estimate holds after interchanging $1$ and $2$. Thus $$\\left|\\log\\frac{\\rho_2(q)}{\\rho_1(q)}\\right|\n       \\le C_HM^2\\delta_K e^{\\zeta_HK},\\qquad q\\in G.\n \\label{cmp:relative-density}$$ There are also $c_H,d_H>0$ and $K_H<\\infty$ such that $$\\left|\\log\\frac{Z_2}{Z_1}\\right|\\le C_He^{-d_HK}\n \\quad\\text{if}\\quad\n K\\ge K_H,\\qquad M_H\\le M,\\qquad M^2\\le e^{c_HK}.\n \\label{cmp:partition-conclusion}$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-cmp-removal-identities",
        "exact_quote": "The background left after selecting ${\\cal A}$ has total weight $$\\Xi_i(I\\setminus B_{\\cal A};S_{\\cal A}).\n \\label{cmp:background-identity}$$ Consequently $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\Xi_i(I;S)\n &=\n \\sum_{\\substack{{\\cal A}\\ {\\rm compatible}\\\\\n                   S_U\\cap S\\ne\\varnothing\\ \\forall U\\in{\\cal A}}}\n (-1)^{|{\\cal A}|}\n \\left[\\prod_{U\\in{\\cal A}}w_i(U;q(I))\\right]\n \\Xi_i(I\\setminus B_{\\cal A};S_{\\cal A}),                 \\label{cmp:IE}\\\\\n \\Xi_2(I)-\\Xi_1(I)\n &=\n \\sum_{\\substack{{\\cal A}\\ {\\rm compatible}\\\\{\\cal A}\\ne\\varnothing}}\n \\left[\\prod_{U\\in{\\cal A}}\n       \\bigl(w_2(U;q(I))-w_1(U;q(I))\\bigr)\\right]\n \\Xi_1(I\\setminus B_{\\cal A};S_{\\cal A}).                 \\label{cmp:difference-identity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The empty term in <a href=\"#cmp:IE\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cmp:IE\">[cmp:IE]</a> is $\\Xi_i(I)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $f_1,f_2>0$ be integrable, with $0<Z_i=\\int f_i\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\nu<\\infty$ and $\\pi_i=f_i\\nu/Z_i$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-alignment",
        "exact_quote": "For the actual retained densities of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:rg-closure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:rg-closure\">9</a>, uniformly in the cutoff depth and the admitted terminal coupling, $$\\label{cmp:alignment}\n \\mathbb E|V_X-V_{X+e}|^2\\le\\varepsilon_H,\\qquad\n \\varepsilon_H=(t_H/C_1)^2+4e^{-\\sigma_H}\n                         \\longrightarrow0\\quad(H\\to\\infty).$$ If $H$ is large enough that $\\varepsilon_H\\le1/9$, then $$\\label{cmp:block-correlation}\n \\mathbb E[V_0^{(1)}V_{3e}^{(1)}]\\ge\\frac18.$$ At depth $N$ this is the correlation of two centered original-spin observables of norm at most one, supported in their respective ancestral blocks of side $L^N$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-continuum",
        "exact_quote": "Let $a_j\\downarrow0$ and let $T_j$ be positive lattice transfer contractions with normalized vacua $\\Omega_j$ and full gaps $m_j$ satisfying $\\liminf_jm_j/a_j\\ge m_*>0$. Suppose a limiting theory has Hilbert space $\\mathcal H$, normalized vacuum $\\Omega$, and a strongly continuous semigroup $e^{-t\\mathsf H}$ with $\\mathsf H\\ge0$ self-adjoint and $\\mathsf H\\Omega=0$. Suppose a set of centered vectors $u$ dense in $\\Omega^\\perp$ has centered lattice approximants $u_j\\perp\\Omega_j$ and nonnegative integers $n_j(t)$ such that $$\\left\\lVert u_j\\right\\rVert^2\\to\\left\\lVert u\\right\\rVert^2,\\qquad\n \\langle u_j,T_j^{n_j(t)}u_j\\rangle\n       \\to\\langle u,e^{-t\\mathsf H}u\\rangle,\n \\qquad a_jn_j(t)\\to t$$ for every $t\\ge0$. Then $$\\operatorname{spec}(\\mathsf H)\\subset\\{0\\}\\cup[m_*,\\infty),\\qquad\n \\ker\\mathsf H=\\mathbb C\\Omega.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-continuum",
          "exact_quote": "Let $a_j\\downarrow0$ and let $T_j$ be positive lattice transfer contractions with normalized vacua $\\Omega_j$ and full gaps $m_j$ satisfying $\\liminf_jm_j/a_j\\ge m_*>0$. Suppose a limiting theory has Hilbert space $\\mathcal H$, normalized vacuum $\\Omega$, and a strongly continuous semigroup $e^{-t\\mathsf H}$ with $\\mathsf H\\ge0$ self-adjoint and $\\mathsf H\\Omega=0$. Suppose a set of centered vectors $u$ dense in $\\Omega^\\perp$ has centered lattice approximants $u_j\\perp\\Omega_j$ and nonnegative integers $n_j(t)$ such that $$\\left\\lVert u_j\\right\\rVert^2\\to\\left\\lVert u\\right\\rVert^2,\\qquad\n \\langle u_j,T_j^{n_j(t)}u_j\\rangle\n       \\to\\langle u,e^{-t\\mathsf H}u\\rangle,\n \\qquad a_jn_j(t)\\to t$$ for every $t\\ge0$. Then $$\\operatorname{spec}(\\mathsf H)\\subset\\{0\\}\\cup[m_*,\\infty),\\qquad\n \\ker\\mathsf H=\\mathbb C\\Omega.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-215.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $a_j\\downarrow0$ and let $T_j$ be positive lattice transfer contractions with normalized vacua $\\Omega_j$ and full gaps $m_j$ satisfying $\\liminf_jm_j/a_j\\ge m_*>0$. Suppose a limiting theory has Hilbert space $\\mathcal H$, normalized vacuum $\\Omega$, and a strongly continuous semigroup $e^{-t\\mathsf H}$ with $\\mathsf H\\ge0$ self-adjoint and $\\mathsf H\\Omega=0$. Suppose a set of centered vectors $u$ dense in $\\Omega^\\perp$ has centered lattice approximants $u_j\\perp\\Omega_j$ and nonnegative integers $n_j(t)$ such that $$\\left\\lVert u_j\\right\\rVert^2\\to\\left\\lVert u\\right\\rVert^2,\\qquad\n \\langle u_j,T_j^{n_j(t)}u_j\\rangle\n       \\to\\langle u,e^{-t\\mathsf H}u\\rangle,\n \\qquad a_jn_j(t)\\to t$$ for every $t\\ge0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-app-free-strip",
        "exact_quote": "For the sampled smooth block weights in Section <a href=\"#free:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"free:section\">4</a>, there are $\\delta,c,C>0$ and $L_0$ with the following properties for $L\\ge L_0$ and $h\\ge0$. The precisions $K_h$ and an elliptic edge lift $B_h$ are analytic on the complex $\\delta$-neighborhood of the momentum torus. On the real torus, $$cD\\le K_h\\le CD,\\qquad cI\\le B_h\\le CI,\n \\qquad K_h=d^*B_hd,\\quad B_h(0)=I.$$ Their fixed derivatives are bounded, $K_h-D$ has a zero of order four at zero, and their history differences, including the inverse edge lifts, are bounded by $CL^{-h}$ when compared with any depth $\\bar h\\ge h$. The same assertions hold on a common smaller strip.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-app-free-strip",
          "exact_quote": "For the sampled smooth block weights in Section <a href=\"#free:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"free:section\">4</a>, there are $\\delta,c,C>0$ and $L_0$ with the following properties for $L\\ge L_0$ and $h\\ge0$. The precisions $K_h$ and an elliptic edge lift $B_h$ are analytic on the complex $\\delta$-neighborhood of the momentum torus. On the real torus, $$cD\\le K_h\\le CD,\\qquad cI\\le B_h\\le CI,\n \\qquad K_h=d^*B_hd,\\quad B_h(0)=I.$$ Their fixed derivatives are bounded, $K_h-D$ has a zero of order four at zero, and their history differences, including the inverse edge lifts, are bounded by $CL^{-h}$ when compared with any depth $\\bar h\\ge h$. The same assertions hold on a common smaller strip.",
          "source_id": "PM-215.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the sampled smooth block weights in Section <a href=\"#free:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"free:section\">4</a>, there are $\\delta,c,C>0$ and $L_0$ with the following properties for $L\\ge L_0$ and $h\\ge0$. The precisions $K_h$ and an elliptic edge lift $B_h$ are analytic on the complex $\\delta$-neighborhood of the momentum torus. On the real torus, $$cD\\le K_h\\le CD,\\qquad cI\\le B_h\\le CI,\n \\qquad K_h=d^*B_hd,\\quad B_h(0)=I.$$ Their fixed derivatives are bounded, $K_h-D$ has a zero of order four at zero, and their history differences, including the inverse edge lifts, are bounded by $CL^{-h}$ when compared with any depth $\\bar h\\ge h$. The same assertions hold on a common smaller strip.",

        "anchor": "stmt-app-alias-division",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose an analytic alias vector $R_l(p)$ satisfies $d(k_l)^*R_l(p)=0$, has the bounds $$|R_l(p)|\\le C_sL^{-1}\\langle l\\rangle^{-s}$$ on a fixed larger domain for every required fixed $s$, and has a zero through first order at the principal origin. Then $R_l=c(k_l)^*\\gamma_l$ on a smaller fixed domain, with $|\\gamma_l|\\le C_s\\langle l\\rangle^{-s}$ and $\\gamma_0(0)=0$. If $R_l(0)=0$ on every nonprincipal alias, then $\\gamma_l(0)=0$ for all $l$.*\n\n*If, in addition, $\\gamma_l(0)=0$ for every alias, there is an analytic row $H_l$ satisfying $H_ld'(p)=\\gamma_l(p)$ and $|H_l|\\le C_s\\langle l\\rangle^{-s}$.         "anchor": "stmt-supp-one-norm-contraction",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\sigma\\le c_*/2$. Under <a href=\"#supp:fixed-norm-kernel-assumption\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"supp:fixed-norm-kernel-assumption\">[supp:fixed-norm-kernel-assumption]</a>, substitution <a href=\"#supp:fixed-norm-substitution\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"supp:fixed-norm-substitution\">[supp:fixed-norm-substitution]</a> has the following bounds in the single norm just defined: $$\\|A'_n\\|_\\sigma\\le C L^{2-n}\\|A_n\\|_\\sigma\n       \\quad(3\\le n\\le6),\\qquad\n \\|A'_2\\|_\\sigma\\le \\frac C L\\|A_2\\|_\\sigma.\n \\label{supp:one-norm-conclusion}$$ The quadratic assertion is for sums of compensated packets and includes the cost of output compensation. To regard this compensation as a representation of the same polynomial, assume additionally <a href=\"#supp:fixed-norm-affine-assumption\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"supp:fixed-norm-affine-assumption\">[supp:fixed-norm-affine-assumption]</a> and that the output polynomial at each coarse anchor is invariant under the square symmetries. The latter holds for bulk translation-invariant input coefficients and a block-centered, square-equivariant kernel $P$. In this case the entire transferred quadratic polynomial has zero affine Hessian, and its stiffness extraction is exactly zero.*\n\n*The constant $C$ and a lower threshold for $L$ depend only on $\\sigma$, $c_*$, $K_1$, $K_2$, and the fixed tensor conventions. They do not depend on $L$, the history, or the coefficient lists.         "anchor": "stmt-app-principal-log",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that all internal edge defaults of an $L$-block and its observation default are retained in a product. On the support of this product, or of any product-rule term of a fixed-order derivative of its smooth profiles, every constituent spin satisfies $$|q_xV_Y^{-1}-1|\\le K_Lt.$$ If $q_x=\\exp(\\xi_x)V_Y$ and $|\\xi_x|<\\pi$, then $\\xi_x$ is the principal logarithm and $|\\xi_x|\\le 2K_Lt$ for sufficiently large $H$.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-215.4"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-app-local-marginals",
        "exact_quote": "The covariance $C_P(x,y)$ vanishes for $d(x,y)>R$. For $I\\subset E(P)$ its marginal matrix $C_P[I,I]$ depends only on $P\\cap N_{R+3M}(I)$. Its numerical mean on $I$ also depends only on the mean windows, affine-row field arguments, and hole queries specified above.*\n\n*Suppose finitely many prepared factors have disjoint complete supports. Their Gaussian coordinate sets have distance greater than $R$, their frozen-spin and tag argument sets are disjoint, and their integrals factor exactly. Each individual integral is unchanged on deleting outside primary components whose complete supports are disjoint from its own, with all recorded external fields and status answers fixed.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-supp-gaussian-product",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a centered Gaussian vector with covariance $C$, where $0\\le C\\le c_*I$ and $c_*>0$. Let $O_i$ satisfy $|O_i|\\le a_i$. For a finite collection of ratio factors put $$R_\\alpha=e^{Q_\\alpha}-1,\\qquad\n |Q_\\alpha(Z)|\\le e_\\alpha\n       \\left(1+\\sum_{x\\in I_\\alpha}|Z_x|^2\\right),\n \\qquad 0<e_\\alpha\\le1.$$ Suppose that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_x\\sum_{\\alpha:x\\in I_\\alpha}\\sqrt{e_\\alpha}\n       &\\le(64c_*)^{-1},\\label{supp:endpoint-load}\\\\\n (2+4c_*|I_\\alpha|)\\sqrt{e_\\alpha}\n       &\\le\\tfrac14\\log(e_\\alpha^{-1})\n       \\quad\\hbox{for every }\\alpha.\\label{supp:ratio-size}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Let $F_1,\\ldots,F_n$ be bounded factors whose product has absolute value at most $\\mathbf1_E$, where $\\mathbb P(E)\\le e^{-\\tau n}$. Finally suppose $\\|S\\|_{L^4}\\le B$. Then $$\\mathbb E\\left|S\\prod_iO_i\\prod_\\alpha R_\\alpha\n                         \\prod_{j=1}^nF_j\\right|\n \\le B\\prod_i a_i\\prod_\\alpha e_\\alpha^{1/4}\n                                      e^{-\\tau n/2}.$$ The convention for $n=0$ is $E$ equal to the full probability space. The conclusion also holds for factors $R_\\alpha$ satisfying directly $|R_\\alpha|\\le\\sqrt{e_\\alpha}\\exp\\{2\\sqrt{e_\\alpha}\n(1+\\sum_{x\\in I_\\alpha}|Z_x|^2)\\}$. Here $x$ indexes scalar Gaussian coordinates; vector coordinates may be replaced by their scalar components. No independence between any of the displayed factors is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-supp-gaussian-product",

Finally suppose $\\|S\\|_{L^4}\\le B$. Then $$\\mathbb E\\left|S\\prod_iO_i\\prod_\\alpha R_\\alpha\n                         \\prod_{j=1}^nF_j\\right|\n \\le B\\prod_i a_i\\prod_\\alpha e_\\alpha^{1/4}\n                                      e^{-\\tau n/2}.$$ The convention for $n=0$ is $E$ equal to the full probability space. The conclusion also holds for factors $R_\\alpha$ satisfying directly $|R_\\alpha|\\le\\sqrt{e_\\alpha}\\exp\\{2\\sqrt{e_\\alpha}\n(1+\\sum_{x\\in I_\\alpha}|Z_x|^2)\\}$. Here $x$ indexes scalar Gaussian coordinates; vector coordinates may be replaced by their scalar components. No independence between any of the displayed factors is required.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $Z$ be a centered Gaussian vector with covariance $C$, where $0\\le C\\le c_*I$ and $c_*>0$. Let $O_i$ satisfy $|O_i|\\le a_i$. For a finite collection of ratio factors put $$R_\\alpha=e^{Q_\\alpha}-1,\\qquad\n |Q_\\alpha(Z)|\\le e_\\alpha\n       \\left(1+\\sum_{x\\in I_\\alpha}|Z_x|^2\\right),\n \\qquad 0<e_\\alpha\\le1.$$ Suppose that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_x\\sum_{\\alpha:x\\in I_\\alpha}\\sqrt{e_\\alpha}\n       &\\le(64c_*)^{-1},\\label{supp:endpoint-load}\\\\\n (2+4c_*|I_\\alpha|)\\sqrt{e_\\alpha}\n       &\\le\\tfrac14\\log(e_\\alpha^{-1})\n       \\quad\\hbox{for every }\\alpha.\\label{supp:ratio-size}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Let $F_1,\\ldots,F_n$ be bounded factors whose product has absolute value at most $\\mathbf1_E$, where $\\mathbb P(E)\\le e^{-\\tau n}$. The conclusion also holds for factors $R_\\alpha$ satisfying directly $|R_\\alpha|\\le\\sqrt{e_\\alpha}\\exp\\{2\\sqrt{e_\\alpha}\n(1+\\sum_{x\\in I_\\alpha}|Z_x|^2)\\}$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-supp-product-reserve",
        "exact_quote": "Let the factors in Lemma <a href=\"#supp:gaussian-product\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"supp:gaussian-product\">38</a> carry integer loads $s\\ge1$, and let $s_{\\rm tot}$ be their total load. Fix an integer $k$. Suppose that each differentiated product, through order $k$, is a sum of at most $C_k(1+s_{\\rm tot})^{d_k}$ expressions to which that lemma applies. Each expression retains the original factor indices and envelopes $a_i,e_\\alpha,I_\\alpha$, the same failure count $n$, and the same core loads; differentiated factors may use the direct ratio envelope stated in that lemma. Suppose, for every such expression, $$B\\le C_k g^{-d_k}(1+s_{\\rm tot})^{d_k},\\qquad 0<g<1.$$ Suppose each expression requiring the factor $g^{-d_k}$ has at least one of the following reserves:*\n\n1.  *a deterministic core factor bounded by $e^{-\\kappa p^2s_c}$, with $\\kappa p^2/2\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$;*\n\n2.  *at least one failure test, with $\\tau/4\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$;*\n\n3.  *at least one ratio factor, with $\\log(e_\\alpha^{-1})/8\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$;*\n\n4.  *a deterministic exceptional factor with envelope $a_*\\le e_*$, where $0<e_*<1$ and $\\log(e_*^{-1})/2\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$.*\n\n*Expressions without any such factor are assumed to obey the same bound on $B$ without $g^{-d_k}$. Then the derivative has a joint product majorant with deterministic ordinary weights $a_i$, core weights $e^{-\\kappa p^2s_c/2}$, failure weights $e^{-\\tau/4}$, and ratio weights $e_\\alpha^{1/8}$, and deterministic exceptional weights $e_*^{1/2}$ replacing their original envelopes, times a fixed polynomial in $1+s_{\\rm tot}$. For a single marked parameter or input discrepancy, factor out its size and use an envelope for the remaining marked factor in place of its original envelope. No bound relative to the original factor is required; that factor may vanish while its discrepancy does not. If the hypotheses above, including the reserves, hold with this replacement, and all other envelopes hold uniformly along the comparison or interpolation, the same conclusion retains the discrepancy size.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-supp-product-reserve",
          "exact_quote": "Let the factors in Lemma <a href=\"#supp:gaussian-product\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"supp:gaussian-product\">38</a> carry integer loads $s\\ge1$, and let $s_{\\rm tot}$ be their total load. Fix an integer $k$. Suppose that each differentiated product, through order $k$, is a sum of at most $C_k(1+s_{\\rm tot})^{d_k}$ expressions to which that lemma applies. Each expression retains the original factor indices and envelopes $a_i,e_\\alpha,I_\\alpha$, the same failure count $n$, and the same core loads; differentiated factors may use the direct ratio envelope stated in that lemma. Suppose, for every such expression, $$B\\le C_k g^{-d_k}(1+s_{\\rm tot})^{d_k},\\qquad 0<g<1.$$ Suppose each expression requiring the factor $g^{-d_k}$ has at least one of the following reserves:*\n\n1.  *a deterministic core factor bounded by $e^{-\\kappa p^2s_c}$, with $\\kappa p^2/2\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$;*\n\n2.  *at least one failure test, with $\\tau/4\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$;*\n\n3.  *at least one ratio factor, with $\\log(e_\\alpha^{-1})/8\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$;*\n\n4.  *a deterministic exceptional factor with envelope $a_*\\le e_*$, where $0<e_*<1$ and $\\log(e_*^{-1})/2\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$.*\n\n*Expressions without any such factor are assumed to obey the same bound on $B$ without $g^{-d_k}$. Then the derivative has a joint product majorant with deterministic ordinary weights $a_i$, core weights $e^{-\\kappa p^2s_c/2}$, failure weights $e^{-\\tau/4}$, and ratio weights $e_\\alpha^{1/8}$, and deterministic exceptional weights $e_*^{1/2}$ replacing their original envelopes, times a fixed polynomial in $1+s_{\\rm tot}$. For a single marked parameter or input discrepancy, factor out its size and use an envelope for the remaining marked factor in place of its original envelope. No bound relative to the original factor is required; that factor may vanish while its discrepancy does not. If the hypotheses above, including the reserves, hold with this replacement, and all other envelopes hold uniformly along the comparison or interpolation, the same conclusion retains the discrepancy size.",
          "source_id": "PM-215.4"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let the factors in Lemma <a href=\"#supp:gaussian-product\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"supp:gaussian-product\">38</a> carry integer loads $s\\ge1$, and let $s_{\\rm tot}$ be their total load. Fix an integer $k$. Suppose that each differentiated product, through order $k$, is a sum of at most $C_k(1+s_{\\rm tot})^{d_k}$ expressions to which that lemma applies. Each expression retains the original factor indices and envelopes $a_i,e_\\alpha,I_\\alpha$, the same failure count $n$, and the same core loads; differentiated factors may use the direct ratio envelope stated in that lemma. Suppose, for every such expression, $$B\\le C_k g^{-d_k}(1+s_{\\rm tot})^{d_k},\\qquad 0<g<1.$$ Suppose each expression requiring the factor $g^{-d_k}$ has at least one of the following reserves:*\n\n1.  *a deterministic core factor bounded by $e^{-\\kappa p^2s_c}$, with $\\kappa p^2/2\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$;*\n\n2.  *at least one failure test, with $\\tau/4\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$;*\n\n3.  *at least one ratio factor, with $\\log(e_\\alpha^{-1})/8\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$;*\n\n4.  *a deterministic exceptional factor with envelope $a_*\\le e_*$, where $0<e_*<1$ and $\\log(e_*^{-1})/2\\ge d_k\\log(g^{-1})$.*\n\n*Expressions without any such factor are assumed to obey the same bound on $B$ without $g^{-d_k}$. Then the derivative has a joint product majorant with deterministic ordinary weights $a_i$, core weights $e^{-\\kappa p^2s_c/2}$, failure weights $e^{-\\tau/4}$, and ratio weights $e_\\alpha^{1/8}$, and deterministic exceptional weights $e_*^{1/2}$ replacing their original envelopes, times a fixed polynomial in $1+s_{\\rm tot}$. For a single marked parameter or input discrepancy, factor out its size and use an envelope for the remaining marked factor in place of its original envelope. No bound relative to the original factor is required; that factor may vanish while its discrepancy does not. If the hypotheses above, including the reserves, hold with this replacement, and all other envelopes hold uniformly along the comparison or interpolation, the same conclusion retains the discrepancy size.",
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **mandatory covering sum** ([source](#stmt-cmp-cover-definition)): A label $\lambda$ consists of a finite site support $P_\lambda$, a load $s_\lambda\ge1$, and a fixed nonempty inventory $J_\lambda$ of bonds whose endpoints belong to $P_\lambda$.
- **compatible family** ([source](#cmp:cover)): A family is compatible when its supports are pairwise disjoint.
- **kinetic coupling** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The *kinetic coupling* is the bulk coefficient extracted from the quadratic cost of a slowly varying spin configuration in that action, in the same inverse-coupling convention as $\beta$.
- **terminal coupling** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The *terminal coupling* is its value when blocking stops.
- **natural inverse length** ([source](#eq:mass-definition)): We abbreviate the natural inverse length by $\rho(\beta)=\sqrt\beta e^{-\pi\beta}$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-sharp-gap-scale | full transfer gap of the periodic O(4) model | ~ | $\sqrt\beta\,e^{-\pi\beta}$ | bare inverse coupling at least the threshold |
| q-box-threshold | square-box defect threshold | = | $2^{-10}$ | fixed inverse coupling |
| q-criterion-gap | full gap under the small-defect criterion | ≥ | $\frac{\log2}{n}$ | side n with defect below threshold |
| q-defect-squaring | time-doubled defect | ≤ | $4p(n,w)^2$ | small defect |
| q-defect-recursion | square-box defect after doubling | ≤ | $64\Delta_\beta(r)^2$ | defect below threshold |
| q-drift-constant | kinetic drift per block step | = | $(\log L)/\pi$ | fixed dyadic blocking factor |
| q-depth-calibration | depth times log of blocking factor | = | $\pi\beta-\tfrac12\log\beta+O_{L,H}(1)$ | terminal coupling in the fixed interval |
| q-lower-depth-gap | full gap at depth N | ≥ | $\frac{\log2}{M_0L^N}$ | depth at least the reference depth |
| q-block-correlation | correlation of one spin component at fixed separation | ≥ | $\frac18$ | uniform in depth and terminal coupling |
| q-upper-depth-gap | full gap at depth N | ≤ | $(\log8)L^{-N}$ | all depths |
