We apply Lemma <a href="#supp:gaussian-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="supp:gaussian-product">38</a> on the union of the read coordinates of a finite selection in one fixed primary pattern. The scalar-coordinate covariance bound follows from <a href="#rg:window-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:window-bounds">[rg:window-bounds]</a>; it is uniform in the pattern, its exterior, and the admitted period. Empty exterior means a zero-dimensional Gaussian and causes no exception. The complete read sets and attached profile rules are those of Appendix <a href="#app:rg-geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:rg-geometry">14</a>.

For the eight classes listed in Section <a href="#rg:prepared" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rg:prepared">5.3</a>, the following are the required envelopes and reserves.

1.  The unassigned nonlinear rows have deterministic envelopes $C_Lg\operatorname{poly}(M,p)$. Their normalized smooth derivatives retain that order: a cutoff derivative contributes $t^{-1}$ together with a direction of size $t$ times its prescribed distance weight.

2.  The stationary squares have order $g^2$; the $\mathcal N,\mathcal Z$ remainders have exponential accuracy in $M$. Their fixed-order derivatives have the same envelopes by <a href="#rg:prediction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:prediction">[rg:prediction]</a> and the truncated free-kernel estimates.

3.  A remote canonical term retains <a href="#rg:old-polynomial-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:old-polynomial-bound">[rg:old-polynomial-bound]</a>, and a remote regular error retains its actual derivative norm. A nonremote term is a selected hard letter with the attached profiles prescribed in Appendix <a href="#app:rg-geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:rg-geometry">14</a>; it meets a compulsory core by its recorded nonremote test.

4.  A kinetic tail retains every path and mask query. If it is smooth its exponential envelope suffices. If it is treated as a hard letter, that same exceptional envelope pays its fixed-order transport loss. The preparation is $1+\chi_{\rm att}(e^V-1)$, so it introduces no unpriced cutoff failure.

5.  The ratio-chain letters use <a href="#rg:ratio-envelope" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:ratio-envelope">[rg:ratio-envelope]</a>. Their scalar endpoint counts are polynomial in their recorded loads and in $M$. To sum their eighth roots, fix a support exponent $B>0$, a support hit, and a number $r\ge1$ of residual hops. The finite-range convention of Section <a href="#app:factor-ownership" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:factor-ownership">14.1</a> gives $O(M)$ range for each matrix hop and window. There are at most $C_LM^D$ choices at each hop; choosing which of the $O(r)$ retained windows has its fixed $O(M)$ support neighborhood containing the hit costs only a further polynomial. Thus the number $N_r$ of literal paths is at most $$N_r\le\operatorname{poly}(M,r)(C_LM^D)^{r+O(1)}.$$ For a resolvent chain, first integrate its coefficient in the resolvent parameter, using the integrable $(1+u)^{-2}$ bound in Section <a href="#rg:gaussian" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rg:gaussian">5.2</a>. The envelopes $e_\alpha$ then index discrete paths. Apply <a href="#rg:chain-price" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:chain-price">[rg:chain-price]</a> with support exponent $8B$ and Hölder’s inequality for this finite path set to obtain $$\begin{aligned}
     \sum_{\alpha:\,r\text{ hops}}e^{Bs_\alpha}e_\alpha^{1/8}
     &\le N_r^{7/8}
       \left(\sum_{\alpha:\,r\text{ hops}}
                      e^{8Bs_\alpha}e_\alpha\right)^{1/8}\\
     &\le\operatorname{poly}(M,r)
         \exp\{-c_LMr/8+O_L(r\log M)+CBr\}.
     \end{aligned}$$ This is summable over $r$ and tends to zero as $H$ increases. Since the envelopes are less than one, their square-root sum has the same upper bound. Increasing $H$ makes this support-hit sum less than $(64c_*)^{-1}$. The exponential decrease in $M$ times the number of hops dominates the polynomial endpoint count, proving <a href="#supp:ratio-size" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="supp:ratio-size">[supp:ratio-size]</a> as well.

6.  The Haar-Jacobian letter is smooth and has order $g^2$ with its normalized derivatives, since the Jacobian has zero linear term.

7.  The opened edge, observation and chart defaults are the failure factors. Equation <a href="#rg:joint-rarity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:joint-rarity">[rg:joint-rarity]</a> supplies the simultaneous bound with $\tau=c_Lp^2/M^D$. It holds on all fixed-order derivative supports, by the prediction estimates and the retained support properties of Section <a href="#rg:prepared" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rg:prepared">5.3</a>.

8.  An old covering weight retains its sup envelope. Its nonempty inventory supplies a marked primary input edge, hence a compulsory core. Its measurable physical-spin arguments are fixed by the common transport; the weight is never field-differentiated.

The compulsory core itself is <a href="#app:explicit-core" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="app:explicit-core">[app:explicit-core]</a>. Its reserve, proved there on the retained profile and derivative supports, gives a deterministic envelope $e^{-\kappa_Lp^2s_c}$ after increasing $H$. Normalized derivatives of its smooth factors or exponent cost only a fixed polynomial in $g^{-1},M,p$ and the total number of reads. The same reserve remains during interpolation of two core exponents at fixed physical spins, because both endpoints obey it.

For a smooth product without a hard factor, the ordinary derivative bounds already stated preserve the ordinary orders. In a product with hard physical-spin dependencies, use the single simultaneous transport at the end of Appendix <a href="#app:rg-geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:rg-geometry">14</a>. That calculation supplies the derivatives of the mean, covariance score, vector field and divergence required above. There are at most a polynomial number of placements at a fixed total derivative order. The $L^4$ norm of each score is bounded by a fixed polynomial of the number of reads and by fixed powers of $g^{-1},M,p$. Fixed powers of $M,p$ can be absorbed in a further fixed power of $g^{-1}$ for sufficiently large $H$. Every use that requires this loss has a core, failure or exceptional ratio/tail reserve by the eight cases just listed. A marked coupling, history or input discrepancy retains its own factor in these same bounds, using the window difference identity and the interpolation at fixed physical-spin arguments. For an input discrepancy, telescope a finite product into terms with one marked factor $F_{\alpha,2}-F_{\alpha,1}$. Its coefficient, regular-error or covering-weight difference supplies the replacement envelope in Corollary <a href="#supp:product-reserve" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="supp:product-reserve">39</a>; the other factors use envelopes uniform for the two inputs. The marked support retains the same core or exceptional reserve whenever transport requires it.

Corollary <a href="#supp:product-reserve" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="supp:product-reserve">39</a> therefore proves <a href="#rg:joint-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:joint-product">[rg:joint-product]</a>. The support-hit sums of the resulting majorants are obtained exactly as at the end of the proof of Lemma <a href="#rg:joint" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rg:joint">11</a>: projected old records use <a href="#rg:projection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:projection">[rg:projection]</a>, canonical paths use their fixed exponential weight, core patterns are paid by their $p^2$ reserve, and chains use <a href="#rg:chain-price" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:chain-price">[rg:chain-price]</a>. Reserve a further fixed positive amount of support exponent for the polynomial in total load. The conditions <a href="#rg:finite-choices" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rg:finite-choices">[rg:finite-choices]</a> leave this reserve while retaining $e^{-p^{3/10}}$ for output-seeded components. This is a product estimate under one Gaussian, not an independence assertion about overlapping factors. Exact independence is used only for disjoint complete supports, where Lemma <a href="#app:local-marginals" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:local-marginals">37</a> proves it.

      "caption": "The two coordinate directions enter the square-box test. Every rectangle has periodic boundary conditions; the dashed lines mark its dimensions, not cuts that make the pieces independent. The second doubling starts from the enlarged rectangle. The displayed identity is exact, with the fixed coupling suppressed.",
      "figure_id": "fig:rectangle-doubling",
      "pdf_page": 10,
      "source_evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "The two coordinate directions enter the square-box test.\n  Every rectangle has periodic boundary conditions; the dashed lines\n  mark its dimensions, not cuts that make the pieces independent.\n  The second doubling starts from the enlarged r",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=0.92cm,y=0.92cm,font=\\small]\n    \\draw[fill=black!4] (0,0) rectangle (1.5,1.5);\n    \\node at (0.75,0.75) {$Z(n,n)$};\n    \\node[below] at (0.75,0) {$n$};\n    \\node[left] at (0,0.75) {$n$};\n\n    \\draw[->,thick] (1.85,0.75) -- (3.05,0.75);\n    \\node[align=center,above] at (2.45,0.9) {double\\\\time};\n\n    \\draw[fill=black!4] (3.4,0) rectangle (6.4,1.5);\n    \\draw[densely dashed,black!45] (4.9,0) -- (4.9,1.5);\n    \\node[fill=black!4] at (4.9,0.75) {$Z(2n,n)$};\n    \\node[below] at (4.9,0) {$2n$};\n\n    \\draw[->,thick] (6.75,0.75) -- (7.95,0.75);\n    \\node[align=center,above] at (7.35,0.9) {double\\\\space};\n\n    \\draw[fill=black!4] (8.3,0) rectangle (11.3,3);\n    \\draw[densely dashed,black!45] (8.3,1.5) -- (11.3,1.5);\n    \\node at (9.8,0.75) {$Z(2n,2n)$};\n    \\node[below] at (9.8,0) {$2n$};\n    \\node[right] at (11.3,1.5) {$2n$};\n\n    \\node at (5.65,-1.0)\n      {$\\displaystyle\n        \\Delta(n)=2\\underbrace{\\bigl[2\\log Z(n,n)-\\log Z(2n,n)\\bigr]}_{p(n,n)}\n          +\\underbrace{\\bigl[2\\log Z(2n,n)-\\log Z(2n,2n)\\bigr]}_{q(2n,n)}$};\n  \\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Three rectangular boxes arranged horizontally with arrows representing doubling steps:\n- First box: square of size $n \\times n$ labeled $Z(n,n)$, with height dimension $n$ on the left and width dimension $n$ on the bottom.\n- An arrow labeled \"double time\" points from the first box to the second box.\n- Second box: horizontal rectangle of size $2n \\times n$ labeled $Z(2n,n)$, with width dimension $2n$ on the bottom.\n- An arrow labeled \"double space\" points from the second box to the third box.\n- Third box: square of size $2n \\times 2n$ labeled $Z(2n,2n)$, with height dimension $2n$ on the right and width dimension $2n$ on the bottom.\n- Below the boxes is the displayed equation:\n$$\\Delta(n) = 2 \\underbrace{[2\\log Z(n,n) - \\log Z(2n,n)]}_{p(n,n)} + \\underbrace{[2\\log Z(2n,n) - \\log Z(2n,2n)]}_{q(2n,n)}$$"
    },
    {
      "caption": "The comparison aligns the last $K$ blocking steps of two admitted trajectories with $N ≥ K$. Here $b_{K}^{(N)}$ is the longer trajectory’s coupling after its first $N − K$ steps. Their kinetic couplings agree at the terminal scale. Their complete endpoint laws need not agree: contraction bounds the discrepancy in their local interaction data, while bulk scalars are tracked separately. The bound is uniform in the number $N − K$ of earlier steps.",
      "figure_id": "fig:matched-trajectories",
      "pdf_page": 64,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:matched-trajectories",
          "exact_quote": "The comparison aligns the last $K$ blocking steps of two\n  admitted trajectories with $N\\ge K$. Here $b_K^{(N)}$ is the longer\n  trajectory's coupling after its first $N-K$ steps. Their kinetic couplings agree\n  at the terminal scale. Their",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,font=\\small]\n    \\node[anchor=east] at (0,1.7) {depth $N$};\n    \\node[draw,rounded corners,inner sep=5pt] at (1.0,1.7)\n      {$\\beta_N(h_0)$};\n    \\draw[->] (1.9,1.7) -- (3.55,1.7);\n    \\node[above,align=center] at (2.7,1.75) {$N-K$\\\\steps};\n    \\node[draw,rounded corners,inner sep=5pt] at (4.15,1.7)\n      {$b_K^{(N)}$};\n    \\draw[->,thick] (4.85,1.7) -- (8.05,1.7);\n    \\node[above] at (6.45,1.78) {last $K$ steps};\n    \\node[draw,rounded corners,inner sep=5pt] at (8.5,1.7) {$h_0$};\n\n    \\node[anchor=east] at (0,0) {depth $K$};\n    \\node[draw,rounded corners,inner sep=5pt] at (4.15,0)\n      {$\\beta_K(h_0)$};\n    \\draw[->,thick] (5.05,0) -- (8.05,0);\n    \\node[below] at (6.5,-0.08) {$K$ steps};\n    \\node[draw,rounded corners,inner sep=5pt] at (8.5,0) {$h_0$};\n\n    \\draw[densely dashed,black!55] (4.15,0.4) -- (4.15,1.3);\n    \\node[anchor=east,align=right] at (3.9,0.85)\n      {bounded initial\\\\shape discrepancy};\n    \\draw[densely dashed,black!55] (8.5,0.4) -- (8.5,1.3);\n    \\node[anchor=west,align=left] at (8.75,0.85)\n      {same terminal\\\\kinetic coupling};\n\n    \\node[align=center] at (6.2,-1.15)\n      {forward contraction of shape data + terminal coupling condition\\\\[3pt]\n       $\\Longrightarrow$ endpoint interaction discrepancy per anchor $\\le C_H e^{-d_H K}$};\n  \\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A flow diagram comparing two blocking trajectories:\n- Top trajectory labeled \"depth $N$\": node $\\beta_N(h_0)$ connects via an arrow labeled \"$N - K$ steps\" to node $b_K^{(N)}$, which connects via an arrow labeled \"last $K$ steps\" to node $h_0$.\n- Bottom trajectory labeled \"depth $K$\": node $\\beta_K(h_0)$ connects via an arrow labeled \"$K$ steps\" to node $h_0$.\n- Vertical comparisons between trajectories:\n  - A dashed bracket connects the initial segments with annotation \"bounded initial shape discrepancy\".\n  - A vertical line connects the two terminal $h_0$ nodes with annotation \"same terminal kinetic coupling\".\n- Below the trajectories: \"forward contraction of shape data + terminal coupling condition\" pointing via $\\implies$ to \"endpoint interaction discrepancy per anchor $\\le C_H e^{-d_H K}$\"."
    },
    {
      "caption": "The order of choices in the lower-bound argument. The growing family of trial boxes is used only to select one reference depth and one coarse period. The comparison then transfers the small square-box test to all finer admitted cutoffs at the same terminal coupling. The grids are schematic; their counts are not the actual lattice periods. Refinement does not enlarge the physical reference box.",
      "figure_id": "fig:fixed-reference-box",
      "pdf_page": 79,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:fixed-reference-box",
          "exact_quote": "The order of choices in the lower-bound argument.\n  The growing family of trial boxes is used only to select one\n  reference depth and one coarse period. The comparison then transfers\n  the small square-box test to all finer admitted cutoff",
          "source_object": "fig:0003"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1.1cm,y=1cm,font=\\small]\n    \\node[align=center] at (1.4,4.0)\n      {trial boxes $M_K$\\\\$\\log M_K=K^{3/4}+O(1)$};\n    \\draw[->,thick] (3.0,4.0) -- (4.0,4.0);\n    \\node[align=center] at (5.6,4.0)\n      {choose one finite $K_0$\\\\and fix $M_0=M_{K_0}$};\n    \\draw[->,thick] (7.2,4.0) -- (8.15,4.0);\n    \\node[align=center] at (9.65,4.0)\n      {refine the cutoff\\\\$N\\ge K_0$};\n\n    \\begin{scope}[shift={(0,0)}]\n      \\draw[step=0.5,black!25,very thin] (0,0) grid (2.5,2.5);\n      \\draw[thick] (0,0) rectangle (2.5,2.5);\n      \\node[above] at (1.25,2.6) {reference depth $K_0$};\n      \\node[align=center,below] at (1.25,-0.08)\n        {$M_0L^{K_0}$ sites per side\\\\[2pt]$a_{K_0}=\\ell_{\\mathrm{ref}}L^{-K_0}$};\n    \\end{scope}\n    \\draw[->,thick] (2.85,1.25) -- (3.75,1.25);\n    \\begin{scope}[shift={(4.1,0)}]\n      \\draw[step=0.25,black!25,very thin] (0,0) grid (2.5,2.5);\n      \\draw[thick] (0,0) rectangle (2.5,2.5);\n      \\node[above] at (1.25,2.6) {a finer depth $N$};\n      \\node[align=center,below] at (1.25,-0.08)\n        {$M_0L^N$ sites per side\\\\[2pt]$a_N=\\ell_{\\mathrm{ref}}L^{-N}$};\n    \\end{scope}\n    \\draw[->,thick] (6.95,1.25) -- (7.85,1.25);\n    \\begin{scope}[shift={(8.2,0)}]\n      \\draw[step=0.125,black!25,very thin] (0,0) grid (2.5,2.5);\n      \\draw[thick] (0,0) rectangle (2.5,2.5);\n      \\node[above] at (1.25,2.6) {depth $N+1$};\n      \\node[align=center,below] at (1.25,-0.08)\n        {$M_0L^{N+1}$ sites per side\\\\[2pt]$a_{N+1}=\\ell_{\\mathrm{ref}}L^{-(N+1)}$};\n    \\end{scope}\n    \\node at (5.35,-1.45)\n      {Every box has the same physical side $M_0\\ell_{\\mathrm{ref}}$.};\n  \\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A diagram with two rows illustrating selection and refinement of scales:\n- Top row: three boxes connected by horizontal rightward arrows:\n  1. Box containing \"trial boxes $M_K$\" and \"$\\log M_K = K^{3/4} + O(1)$\".\n  2. Arrow to box containing \"choose one finite $K_0$\" and \"and fix $M_0 = M_{K_0}$\".\n  3. Arrow to box containing \"refine the cutoff\" and \"$N \\ge K_0$\".\n- Bottom row: three square grids with increasing mesh density connected by horizontal rightward arrows:\n  1. Coarse square grid labeled above with \"reference depth $K_0$\", and below with \"$M_0 L^{K_0}$ sites per side\" and \"$a_{K_0} = \\ell_{\\mathrm{ref}} L^{-K_0}$\".\n  2. Medium square grid labeled above with \"a finer depth $N$\", and below with \"$M_0 L^N$ sites per side\" and \"$a_N = \\ell_{\\mathrm{ref}} L^{-N}$\".\n  3. Fine square grid labeled above with \"depth $N + 1$\", and below with \"$M_0 L^{N+1}$ sites per side\" and \"$a_{N+1} = \\ell_{\\mathrm{ref}} L^{-(N+1)}$\".\n- Below all three grids is the line: \"Every box has the same physical side $M_0 \\ell_{\\mathrm{ref}}$.\""
    },
    {
      "caption": "Pulling terminal observables back to the original lattice. Each displayed support lies within an ancestral block of side $L^{N}$. The auxiliary block variables are independent between disjoint ancestral blocks conditional on the original spins, so $𝔼[F_{0}F_{3e1}] = 𝔼[V_{0}^{(1)}V_{3e1}^{(1)}]$. Alignment bounds this correlation below by $1/8$; the full transfer gap bounds it above by the exponential of minus the gap times $L^{N}$. No independence of the two pulled-back observables is asserted.",

          "source_object": "fig:0004"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=2.2cm,y=2.2cm,font=\\small]\n    \\draw[fill=black!12,thick] (0,0) rectangle (1,1);\n    \\draw[densely dashed,black!40] (1,0) rectangle (2,1);\n    \\draw[densely dashed,black!40] (2,0) rectangle (3,1);\n    \\draw[fill=black!12,thick] (3,0) rectangle (4,1);\n    \\node[align=center] at (0.5,0.5) {block for $0$\\\\$\\operatorname{supp} F_0$};\n    \\node[align=center] at (3.5,0.5)\n      {block for $3e_1$\\\\$\\operatorname{supp} F_{3e_1}$};\n\n    \\draw[|<->|] (0,1.2) -- (1,1.2);\n    \\node[above] at (0.5,1.2) {$L^N$};\n    \\draw[|<->|] (3,1.2) -- (4,1.2);\n    \\node[above] at (3.5,1.2) {$L^N$};\n    \\draw[|<->|] (1,-0.15) -- (3,-0.15);\n    \\node[below] at (2,-0.15) {support gap $\\ge L^N$};\n    \\draw[->] (-0.15,-0.6) -- (4.25,-0.6);\n    \\node[anchor=west] at (4.25,-0.6) {time};\n\n    \\node at (2,1.85)\n      {$\\displaystyle F_X(\\sigma)=\\mathbb E[V_X^{(1)}\\mid\\sigma],\n       \\qquad |F_X|\\le1,\\quad \\mathbb E F_X=0$};\n  \\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A schematic drawing showing:\n- Top formula: $F_X(\\sigma) = \\mathbb{E}[V_X^{(1)} \\mid \\sigma]$, $|F_X| \\le 1$, $\\mathbb{E} F_X = 0$.\n- Two shaded rectangular blocks of width $L^N$ (marked by dimension arrows $\\longleftrightarrow$ labeled $L^N$ above each):\n  - Left shaded box labeled \"block for 0\" and \"$\\mathrm{supp}\\, F_0$\".\n  - Right shaded box labeled \"block for $3e_1$\" and \"$\\mathrm{supp}\\, F_{3e_1}$\".\n- A dimension arrow between the inner edges of the two boxes labeled \"support gap $\\ge L^N$\".\n- A horizontal arrow pointing to the right beneath the diagram labeled \"$\\to \\mathrm{time}$\"."
    }
  ],
